80.73094pt\raise-18.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ell_{1}(x,y)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.73094pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-20.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{u}\oplus P_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.75783pt\raise-22.25441pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}x^{\overline{\tilde{t}-u}}\\
y^{\overline{v-\tilde{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 70.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{\tilde{t}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

where
a_{1}=(xy)^{q}
is the only possibility compatible with
r>p
, and then, in order for the map to be well-defined, we must have

\ell_{1}=x^{\overline{\tilde{t}-t}}(xy)^{q}
. We must have
q\leq p-1
for the map to be non-zero. This is simply a special case of the map

\alpha_{t,\tilde{t}}
composed with
q
copies of C.1 at the beginning or
q
copies of
C.2
at the end.
The case where the map goes from
P_{v}
to
P_{u}\otimes P_{v}
is entirely dual because again the diagonal map must be zero.

Assume now
Q_{\tilde{t}}=P_{u}\oplus P_{v}
,
Q_{{t}}=P_{u}
,
j>p
. Again all
h=0
as in Case 3, and there are no direct maps from
P_{u}
to
P_{v}
. However, in order to give the most general well-defined map we must have the coefficient of the generator of
P_{v}
vanishing in the upper triangle as it vanishes in the lower triangle, and for this, we need a high power of
xy
in
\ell_{1}(x,y)
, so also in

a_{1}(x,y)
.

\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.30447pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{u}\oplus P_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.30447pt\raise-18.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}(x,y)\\
0\end{pmatrix}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.75783pt\raise 15.0789pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}x^{\overline{\tilde{t}-u}}\\
y^{\overline{v-\tilde{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{\tilde{t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 100.73094pt\raise-18.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ell_{1}(x,y)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 100.73094pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-9.2801pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.3481pt\raise-29.98552pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-4.3478pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{x^{\overline{t-u}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.06601pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 92.06601pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{{t}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

where
a_{1}(x,y)=\sum_{q=0}^{p-1}a^{1}_{q}(xy)^{q}
and
\ell_{1}=\sum_{q_{1}=0}^{p-1}c_{q_{1}}^{1}y^{\overline{\tilde{t}-t}}(xy)^{q_{1}}
. Since
\overline{v-\tilde{t}}+\overline{\tilde{t}-t}=\overline{v-t}
, in order to have the composition of the upper triangle equal to zero, we must have
q_{1}\geq p-\overline{v-t}
. We have
\overline{\tilde{t}-u}>\overline{t-u}
because
t\preceq\tilde{t}
. Now to get the coefficient of the generator of
P_{u}
to be equal in both triangles, we require

a^{1}x^{\overline{t-u}}(xy)^{q}=c^{1}x^{\overline{t-u}}(xy)^{\overline{\tilde{t}-t}+q_{1}}
. The total power of
x
will be less than
p
if
q_{1}<p-\overline{\tilde{t}-u}
. In that case, we take
q=q_{1}+\overline{\tilde{t}-t}
and
a_{q}^{1}=c_{q_{1}}^{1}
. For
q
and
q_{1}
greater than these critical values, the coefficients can be taken arbitrarily. The case with
P_{v}
is dual.

To show that this map can be decomposed as a composition of maps in the catalog, let us start with the case
t=t_{0}=u+(j-p)
and
\tilde{t}=u+(j-p)+1
. In this case,
we have the map in Diagram 2 designated by
\mu_{u}
, where
q_{1}=0
and
\overline{\tilde{t}-t}=1
. We can multiply by a constant to get the general
a_{1}(x,y)
and we can compose with maps of type C.1 or C.2 to get higher values of
q_{1}
. To get more general cases, we use compositions with
\alpha_{t_{0},t}
to get
q=\overline{t_{0}+1-t}
while still having
q_{1}=0
, and then compose with C.1 and C.2. Finally, to get a general value of
\tilde{t}
, we compose with
\alpha_{\tilde{t}.t_{0}+1}
, which increases
q
to
\overline{\tilde{t}-t}
and then compose with an additional
q_{1}
copies of C.1 or C.2.

Case 2.5
: It remains to consider the cases from
P_{w}^{\prime}\to P_{t}^{\prime}
or
P_{t}^{\prime}\to P_{w}^{\prime}
where
w\in I_{0}
,
t\in J
.
If
Q_{t}=P_{u}
, then
j>p
, so we do not have to worry about the sums in Lemma
3.2
.

\textbf{(5.a)}\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern The map
(5.a)
is non-zero if
\overline{u-w}+q<p
and well-defined if
\overline{t-w}+q\geq p
. There is no possibility of a non-zero power of
x
reaching
P_{v}
because
j=\overline{v-u}>p
.
The map
(5.b)
is non-zero if
\overline{u-w}+q<p
and is automatically well-defined. It is not homotopic to zero as long as
q<\overline{t-u}
.
It is irreducible when
q=0
. There is a dual case where we replace
x
by
y
and
u
by
v
.

\textbf{(5.c)}\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.79865pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.14122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-18.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(a_{1},a_{2})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-20.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{u}\oplus P_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.12415pt\raise-22.25441pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}x^{\overline{t-u}}\\
y^{\overline{v-t}}\end{pmatrix}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{t}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\textbf{(5.d)}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.79865pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{u}\oplus P_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-18.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-4.3478pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{x^{\overline{u-w}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.12415pt\raise 15.0789pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}x^{\overline{t-u}}\\
y^{\overline{v-t}}\end{pmatrix}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.79865pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{t}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-10.14122pt\raise-37.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{w}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The map
5.c
will be non-zero when one of the summands in
a_{1}
or
a_{2}
will be non-zero, where

a_{1}(x,y)=\sum_{h=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p-1}{r}\rfloor}\sum_{q_{1}=0}^{(p-1-hr)}a_{q}^{1h}\cdot x^{\overline{u-w}+hr}(xy)^{q}

+\sum_{h=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p-1}{r}\rfloor}\sum_{q^{\prime}_{ii}=0}^{(p-1-hr)}b_{q^{\prime}_{ii}}^{iih}\cdot x^{\overline{u-w}}y^{hr}(xy)^{q^{\prime}_{ii}}

a_{2}(x,y)=1_{\overline{v-u}<p}\sum_{h=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p-1-\overline{v-u}}{r}\rfloor}\sum_{q_{12}=0}^{p-1-(\overline{\tilde{t}-t}+hr)}a_{q_{12}}^{12h}\cdot x^{\overline{v-u}+\overline{u-q}+hr}(xy)^{q_{12}}

+1_{\overline{u-v}<p}\sum_{h=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p-1-\overline{t-\tilde{t}}}{r}\rfloor}\sum_{q^{\prime}_{12}=0}^{(p-1-(\overline{v-u}+hr))}b_{q}^{12h}\cdot y^{\overline{u-v}+\overline{u-w}+hr}(xy)^{q_{21}}

It is well-defined only when the composition of the vertical and horizontal arrows is zero.

In summary, we have found all possible maps between
P_{t}^{\prime}
and
P_{\tilde{t}}^{\prime}
where
u<t,\tilde{t}<v
, and shown how to factor them using intra-arc maps from the catalog.
∎

5
An example of intra-arc maps
:(C_{5}\times C_{5})\rtimes C_{4}

We return to an example we mentioned before in Example
1
with
I=\{0,1,2,3\}
. According to what we did in
\lx@sectionsign

2.5
and Lemma
3.2
, the composition factors of the projective modules form diamonds. This case gives us an example of the extra maps that can appear when
r<p
.
We choose the projective
P_{2}
arbitrarily and make an elementary equivalence with
I_{0}=\{0,1,3\}
. We used a mutation for our first example because the resulting tilting complex is simpler. This gives a tilting complex
T^{\prime}=\bigoplus T^{\prime}_{j}
as in §
2.8

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccc}T_{0}^{\prime}:&&0&\rightarrow&P_{0}&\rightarrow&0\\
T_{1}^{\prime}:&&0&\rightarrow&P_{1}&\rightarrow&0\\
T_{2}^{\prime}:&&0&\rightarrow&P_{1}\oplus P_{3}&\rightarrow&P_{2}&\rightarrow&0\\
T_{3}^{\prime}:&&0&\rightarrow&P_{3}&\rightarrow&0\end{array}

In order to compute the new projective module
P^{\prime}_{2}
in
\operatorname{End}_{D^{b}(A)}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime})
, we want to calculate a basis for
\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}(A)}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime}_{i})
,
i=0,\dots,3
, by finding all the maps between all the projectives, as already done in the previous Proposition
4.1
. Since
r<p
we have a reducible maps involving
x^{4}
from
P_{i}\to P_{i}
and
y^{4}
from
P_{i}\to P_{i}
for
\{i=0,1,3\}
. These maps commute with each other and their product is the socle of the corresponding projective. There are also reducible loops
xy
obtained by going to a neighbor by
x
and returning by
y
, or vice versa. Then
(xy)^{4}
also gives the socle.

Since
J=\{2\}
,
j=2
and
p=5
we are in the case
j\leq p-1
and the adjacent maps come from Diagram 3 as below, where
u=1
,
v=3
all the complexes in the middle section contract to a single line.

\textstyle{P_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{P_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{u}=\begin{pmatrix}xy&-x^{2}\end{pmatrix}}
\textstyle{P_{1}\oplus P_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
id\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}id\\
0\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\binom{x}{y}}
\textstyle{P_{2}}
\textstyle{P_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

We compute the quiver, given in Fig.

.

Figure 5.1:

We now go through the catalog. From
A
, Diagram 3, we have various maps shown between
P^{\prime}_{0},P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{3}
. For chain maps involving
P^{\prime}_{2}
, we let
\ell_{0}
be the term in degree zero and
\ell_{1}
be the term in degree one. We start with maps between
P^{\prime}_{2}
and
P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{3}
given by

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{1}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\epsilon_{3}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{3}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}xy,&-x^{2}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}-y^{2},&xy\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{1},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\pi_{3}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{3},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}id\\
0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}0\\
id\end{array}\right)

The self-maps in
B
are in this case reducible, The map in B.1 is the composition
\epsilon_{1}\circ\pi_{1}
and the map in B.2 is
\epsilon_{3}\circ\pi_{3}
. Since the sum is homotopic to zero, we get a relation that
\epsilon_{1}\circ\pi_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\circ\pi_{3}=0
. Denote by
\gamma
the map from C.2 with
q=1
. It satisfies a relation
\gamma^{5}=0
. It is not reducible because of the contraction of the middle section of Diagram 3 to a single complex
P^{\prime}_{3}
, which means that we cannot go back and forth using
x
and
y
.

From D.1 and D.2 we get self-maps
\eta_{1},\eta_{3}
and from D.3 we get two self-maps
\iota_{1},\iota_{3}
on
P^{\prime}_{2}
, as below

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=x^{4}.

\displaystyle\eta_{3}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=y^{4}.

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}x^{4}&0\\
0&x^{4}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrr}y^{4}&0\\
0&y^{4}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\iota_{1}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=x^{4}.

\displaystyle\iota_{3}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{2}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=y^{4}.

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrr}0&0\\
x^{3}y&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rrrrrr}0&xy^{3}\\
0&0\end{array}\right)

where
\eta_{1}
is the difference between the map in D.1 and the first map in E.3, and
\eta_{3}
is the difference between D.2 and the second map in E.3, while
\iota_{1},\iota_{3}
are the special cases in D.3. We also get a relation, because
\eta_{1}\circ\eta_{3}=\eta_{3}\circ\eta_{1}=\gamma^{4}
.
In addition, from E.4, we have maps from
P^{\prime}_{i}
to
P^{\prime}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\tau_{3}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{3}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\tau_{1}\phantom{{}^{2}}

\displaystyle:P^{\prime}_{1}\to P^{\prime}_{2},\ell_{1}=0.

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}0&y^{4}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\ell_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}x^{4}&0\end{array}\right)

Besides the relations already listed, we can derive relations from
x^{5}=0
,
y^{5}=0
and
xy=yx
, and in addition, we also get
(\gamma)^{5}=0
.
We believe that these are not yet all relations. There is a loop

\epsilon_{3}\circ y^{2}\circ\pi_{1}
and its dual, which may combine with some of the self-maps to give a relation.
